- launchd jobs on the Mac do not inherit the user login PATH: `which cmd` fails over a plain non-login `ssh` *and* inside the `com.adit.node-agent` job, so a homebrew-installed `cmd` and `node` are invisible. An empty `versions.commandcode` string in the heartbeat is a PATH bug, not a missing install — probe with `zsh -lc` (or absolute `/opt/homebrew/bin/...` paths) before concluding the CLI is absent. The mechanism: `/opt/homebrew/bin/cmd` is a **symlink to a `.mjs` file with a `#!/usr/bin/env node` shebang**, so the worker's minimal PATH makes both the version probe and every run fail with `env: node: No such file or directory` even though the binary is present. The fix is a widened `PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/local/bin:$PATH` env applied to **all three** sites — the spawn, the `--version` probe, and the JSON-fallback retry path (mirroring the existing `dshCommandEnv`); fixing only the run leaves the heartbeat still reporting a broken version, and covering only the first two leaves the fallback path broken. This was live-broken on the Mac before it was found; `findBin` already checks `/opt/homebrew/bin`, so the binary resolves fine and the *interpreter* is what's missing. Confidence: 0.8
- **npm `.cmd` shims under a space-containing path cannot be launched from Go at all.** `C:\Program Files\nodejs\cmdc.cmd` is what `exec.LookPath` returns (via PATHEXT), and no quoting scheme survives it once any argument contains a space — which every Kanban prompt does. Three separate "fixes" were built, deployed, and proven wrong in sequence: wrapping in `cmd /c` with a quoted path, `SysProcAttr.CmdLine` (needed because `os/exec` re-escapes `"` in argv into `\"`, but cmd then splits on the space anyway), and flattening embedded newlines (the shim ends in `title %COMSPEC% & ... %*`, so a newline does end the line early — a real but secondary defect). The extensionless sibling `cmdc` is a 423-byte `#!/bin/sh` script, not a Windows executable, so preferring it doesn't help. The working fix is to **skip the shim entirely**: resolve `<shim-dir>/node_modules/<pkg>/dist/index.mjs` and run `node <that file> <args>`. Two traps in that: use the `node.exe` sitting **beside** the shim, not `findBin("node")`, because PATH resolution lands on **Volta's** shim which then hands the CLI an empty query (`No query provided`); and map the shim alias to the real package name (`cmdc` → `command-code`) when scanning `node_modules`. Verified live: `EXIT=0` with a session id. Confirmed again the same day on the dsh side: `node` on Windows PATH resolves to **Volta's** `C:\Program Files\Volta\node.exe`, not the one in `C:\Program Files\nodejs`, while npm's `dsh.cmd` still works because it invokes its sibling `node.exe` by relative path — so anything the worker reaches through `LookPath("node")` lands on Volta. **Volta is still installed there** and was first on PATH; the current mitigation is only to prepend `C:\Program Files\nodejs` ahead of it, so the same version-masking recurs if Volta's PATH entry ever moves ahead again. Removing Volta is still an open decision. Confidence: 0.9
- Redeploying the node-agent to **Windows** needs a five-step dance, because the naive one silently does nothing: (1) `scp` the new build to a **staging** name (`C:\Users\User\node-agent.new.exe`), (2) kill the **supervisor** PowerShell process — the `while ($true)` loop relaunches the agent every 3s, so `Stop-Process node-agent` alone leaves the file locked and the agent back before you look, (3) `schtasks /End /TN NodeAgent` then `Stop-Process node-agent`, (4) `Move-Item -Force` staging → `C:\Users\User\.hermes\bin\node-agent.exe`, (5) rewrite the supervisor wrapper and `schtasks /Create /Run`. The critical part: **running `install-windows.ps1` after a manual `scp` overwrites your copy** with the binary it re-downloads, and its `Move-Item` fails with an `IOException` on the locked file while the script continues anyway — so the old binary keeps serving and the change appears to have no effect. Proof is `(Get-FileHash … -Algorithm MD5).Hash` against the VPS `md5sum` **plus** `LastWriteTime` on the host; two rounds of debugging were wasted because the host timestamp was an hour behind dist. Confidence: 0.8
- The shell you land in over `ssh windows-tailscale` is **bash, not cmd** — `type "C:\Program Files\..."` returns "The network path was not found" and single-quoted PowerShell filters get mangled. Fighting backticks and nested quotes through the ssh boundary burns many round-trips. The reliable form is: write the script to a local file with `write_file`, `scp` it as `C:/Users/User/x.ps1`, then `ssh … 'powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File C:/Users/User/x.ps1'` (forward slashes in the `-File` path; backslashes get eaten). Delete the script from the host afterwards. This is also how a Go/PowerShell reproduction of the exact failing invocation gets run there — building a throwaway `.exe` with `GOOS=windows` and scp'ing it is faster and more faithful than approximating the call through PowerShell. Confidence: 0.8
- Per-node harness CLI version skew is a real deployment hazard: the Mac was on Command Code 0.52.0 (no `--output-format`, no `--resume`) while the Linux host was on 1.66.0, so the exact same worker args passed locally and died on the Mac with `too many arguments`. Check each node's reported `versions.*` before trusting an E2E that only ran on one host, and `cmd --help` on the target to confirm the flag contract rather than inheriting the local build's. That `versions.*` field is also **noisy on Windows**: the version probe captures stdout+stderr together, so node's `ExperimentalWarning` on stderr lands in the string and the reported version is a warning instead of `1.66.0` — cosmetic, but it means a messy version on Windows is a known artifact rather than fresh skew, and a one-line stderr filter tidies it. Confidence: 0.8

The lesson the resolution exposed: a card left green from a previous day is **not** current evidence once anything underneath it has moved — compare the board DB mtime against the run and read the node's `versions.*` before calling a feature verified on that host. Before applying any class from this family, `grep -q "@utility <name> " src/index.css`; and sweep the retired names as their own pass with `grep -rln 'glass-control\|glass-toolbar\|glass-sidebar\|glass-tooltip\|status-pill' src/`, since a dead glass class is invisible in a passing build. Confidence: 0.85
- **The workspace card's "chart" is `EkgTrace`, an inline SVG ping sparkline in `web/src/features/workspaces/WorkspacesPage.tsx`** — 30 samples of latency from a `PingPoint[]`, `ResizeObserver`-driven width, `offsetPath` for the glowing dot. Its colour rule is **one fact per channel**: green = online (a confirmed recent successful ping), red = unreachable *or* a running task, ink = nothing to say. Green was previously reserved for `done` status and the line drew in ink; the matrix background behind it is a `grid-cols-[repeat(15,1fr)]` of one cell per ping. **Workspace "connected" says nothing about whether an agent is working there**, so the running flag comes from a separate query — `GET /api/boards` then `GET /api/boards/<slug>/tasks?status=running` per board, 5s `refetchInterval`, matched on `task.workspace_path` (plus the task id as a fallback). A running task sweeps at 1.4s vs the 2.4s heartbeat so the pace itself distinguishes them. Note the WorkspacesPage has **no `boards` query of its own** and the boards endpoint takes **no `?all=1` param** — verify a route's real query params in `cmd/server/boards_routes.go` before building a query on it. Confidence: 0.8
- **`backdrop-filter` is per-element and unbatchable, and the stylesheet says so in as many words — it is the first suspect for any "the list is laggy" report.** `index.css:521-528` documents it directly: every element carrying one becomes its own compositing layer, the browser cannot batch them, and the file cites **463** of them. `glass-flat` exists as a distinct utility precisely because of that reasoning, and a CSS `transition` carries the same cost (one per row means one compositor layer per row), which is why the select's row highlight is `focus`/`data-[highlighted]` rather than a hover transition. So when list performance is reported, count how many elements *inside the list* carry a blur or a transition — not how many components are involved, and not how many call sites import them. Confidence: 0.8

Caveat: `./ctl.sh status` reported the server stopped while PID 576626 was in fact serving :8788/:8789 (it was never in ctl.sh's pidfile), so confirm the process with `ss -ltnp` and `/proc/<pid>/cwd` before trusting either the status output or a `kill`. node-agent → `./ctl.sh build` then `./ctl.sh restart`, or for a live E2E `go build -o node-agent-server ./cmd/server` and `go build -o agent ./cmd/agent` then restart **both**. An agent can also run ad hoc against the local server with `NODE_AGENT_SERVER=http://127.0.0.1:8788 NODE_AGENT_ID=<id> NODE_AGENT_TOKEN=$(grep -oP '(?<=^NODE_AGENT_TOKEN=).*' ~/.hermes/node-agent.env) ./agent`. On the Mac the node-agent is a launchd agent `com.adit.node-agent` with KeepAlive, so `launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/com.adit.node-agent` restarts it and forces workspace re-registration. - node-agent per shell job used to pay ~7 blocking `postJSON` round trips (4 `emit` progress events + busy/idle heartbeats + result) plus a mandatory 400ms wait in `streamCommand` (loop exits only when `pending == nil`, cleared solely by the 400ms ticker): ~1.65s wall for ~60-80ms of real git work. Fixed via an ordered async `eventEmitter` (queue + single drainer, `Close()` drains before the result post, events batched not dropped) and exiting the stream loop on closure with a synchronous final flush — now ~1.0s. The residual ~1s is the DERP relay, not code. Confidence: 0.75

Confidence: 0.7
- **Disk-space audit recipe for the VPS.** "Why is storage almost full" is answered with evidence, not a guess: `df -h / /home` → `du -xh -d1` top level → drill into the largest dir → reconcile `du -xsh /` against `df` (they matched at 36G vs 37G, i.e. no leak) → then hunt reclaimable candidates by class. Use `-x` throughout so other mounts don't distort the numbers, and pipe through `sort -rh | head -N` to keep output readable. Two probes worth running every time: `lsof -nP | awk '/deleted/ {s+=$8}'` for **deleted-but-open files** (the usual "mystery space" culprit — here 0.5 MB total, proving the space is real), and `sudo -n tune2fs -l /dev/vda2 | grep -i reserved` because **ext4 reserves ~5% for root** (445,577 blocks ≈ 1.7G here), which is why the user sees far less free space than the totals imply. Reclaimable classes to check, in order: `~/.cache/go-build` (220M, regenerable and it refills), stale `node_modules` for apps absent from `pm2 list`, stale `.next`/`dist`/`.turbo` build outputs, deleted-but-still-registered git worktrees (`git worktree list --porcelain | grep -B1 prunable` — a *prunable* entry whose directory is already gone costs only metadata), npm/pnpm caches, and a full `.git` in an install-only clone. Confidence: 0.75
- **Disk-heavy locations on the VPS** (measured at the 40G audit): `~/.hermes` **7.7G** is the single biggest tree — of which `hermes-agent/.git` is **1.4G** (a full clone of `NousResearch/hermes-agent` kept only so the agent can be updated; the largest single reclaimable item, and any change to it touches the *running* agent), `hermes-agent/tools/` is **1.6G** (ffmpeg 512M, python 401M, chromium 364M, node 227M — in use, don't touch), and `hermes-agent/venv` + `.venv` are **813M** for what looks like a duplicate pair in one repo. Elsewhere: `~/apps` 6.8G (mostly `node_modules` — next-portfolio-blog 1.5G, cpns 1.1G, habbit 793M — plus `.next` at 553M and 410M). **Caveat learned during the cleanup: `next-portfolio-blog` is LIVE under pm2** (`pm2 describe` shows `next` running with its exec cwd there, and its 410M `.next` *is* the serving build) — never treat "stale `.next` build output" as a free win without a per-app liveness check; only `cpns-preparation/.next` (553M) turned out to be genuinely dead., `/usr` 7.3G, `/var` 3.8G, `~/.9router` 1.8G, `~/.local`+`~/go`+`~/.nvm` ~2.2G. `~/.nvm` holds only **v24.19.0 (602M)** — an earlier node-22 removal did work, the disk simply refilled. Note the two virtualenvs and the `tools/` payload live *inside* the hermes install, so any "safe to delete" judgement there needs a blast-radius caveat. Confidence: 0.7
- Deploying a rebuilt node-agent to the Mac: the `darwin/arm64` build is cross-compiled on the Linux host (`GOOS=darwin GOARCH=arm64 CGO_ENABLED=0 go build -o dist/node-agent-darwin-arm64 ./cmd/agent`, or `scripts/install-mac.sh`) since the arches differ. The live binary on the Mac is `~/.hermes/bin/node-agent` — `~/apps/node-agent` does not exist there. Then back up the existing binary with a timestamp suffix first, `scp` the build, `chmod +x`, `xattr -c` (quarantine), and `launchctl kickstart -k`. Because a bad build takes the whole agent process down, always check `~/.hermes/node-agent.err.log` for `fatal error`/`panic` afterwards — truncate the log before a soak run so historical panics don't mask new ones. Confidence: 0.85
- launchd jobs on the Mac do not inherit the user login PATH: `which cmd` fails over a plain non-login `ssh` *and* inside the `com.adit.node-agent` job, so a homebrew-installed `cmd` and `node` are invisible. An empty `versions.commandcode` string in the heartbeat is a PATH bug, not a missing install — probe with `zsh -lc` (or absolute `/opt/homebrew/bin/...` paths) before concluding the CLI is absent. The mechanism: `/opt/homebrew/bin/cmd` is a **symlink to a `.mjs` file with a `#!/usr/bin/env node` shebang**, so the worker's minimal PATH makes both the version probe and every run fail with `env: node: No such file or directory` even though the binary is present. The fix is a widened `PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/local/bin:$PATH` env applied to **all three** sites — the spawn, the `--version` probe, and the JSON-fallback retry path (mirroring the existing `dshCommandEnv`); fixing only the run leaves the heartbeat still reporting a broken version, and covering only the first two leaves the fallback path broken. This was live-broken on the Mac before it was found; `findBin` already checks `/opt/homebrew/bin`, so the binary resolves fine and the *interpreter* is what's missing. Confidence: 0.8
- **npm `.cmd` shims under a space-containing path cannot be launched from Go at all.** `C:\Program Files\nodejs\cmdc.cmd` is what `exec.LookPath` returns (via PATHEXT), and no quoting scheme survives it once any argument contains a space — which every Kanban prompt does. Three separate "fixes" were built, deployed, and proven wrong in sequence: wrapping in `cmd /c` with a quoted path, `SysProcAttr.CmdLine` (needed because `os/exec` re-escapes `"` in argv into `\"`, but cmd then splits on the space anyway), and flattening embedded newlines (the shim ends in `title %COMSPEC% & ... %*`, so a newline does end the line early — a real but secondary defect). The extensionless sibling `cmdc` is a 423-byte `#!/bin/sh` script, not a Windows executable, so preferring it doesn't help. The working fix is to **skip the shim entirely**: resolve `<shim-dir>/node_modules/<pkg>/dist/index.mjs` and run `node <that file> <args>`. Two traps in that: use the `node.exe` sitting **beside** the shim, not `findBin("node")`, because PATH resolution lands on **Volta's** shim which then hands the CLI an empty query (`No query provided`); and map the shim alias to the real package name (`cmdc` → `command-code`) when scanning `node_modules`. Verified live: `EXIT=0` with a session id. Confirmed again the same day on the dsh side: `node` on Windows PATH resolves to **Volta's** `C:\Program Files\Volta\node.exe`, not the one in `C:\Program Files\nodejs`, while npm's `dsh.cmd` still works because it invokes its sibling `node.exe` by relative path — so anything the worker reaches through `LookPath("node")` lands on Volta. **Volta is still installed there** and was first on PATH; the current mitigation is only to prepend `C:\Program Files\nodejs` ahead of it, so the same version-masking recurs if Volta's PATH entry ever moves ahead again. Removing Volta is still an open decision. Confidence: 0.9
- Redeploying the node-agent to **Windows** needs a five-step dance, because the naive one silently does nothing: (1) `scp` the new build to a **staging** name (`C:\Users\User\node-agent.new.exe`), (2) kill the **supervisor** PowerShell process — the `while ($true)` loop relaunches the agent every 3s, so `Stop-Process node-agent` alone leaves the file locked and the agent back before you look, (3) `schtasks /End /TN NodeAgent` then `Stop-Process node-agent`, (4) `Move-Item -Force` staging → `C:\Users\User\.hermes\bin\node-agent.exe`, (5) rewrite the supervisor wrapper and `schtasks /Create /Run`. The critical part: **running `install-windows.ps1` after a manual `scp` overwrites your copy** with the binary it re-downloads, and its `Move-Item` fails with an `IOException` on the locked file while the script continues anyway — so the old binary keeps serving and the change appears to have no effect. Proof is `(Get-FileHash … -Algorithm MD5).Hash` against the VPS `md5sum` **plus** `LastWriteTime` on the host; two rounds of debugging were wasted because the host timestamp was an hour behind dist. Confidence: 0.8
- The shell you land in over `ssh windows-tailscale` is **bash, not cmd** — `type "C:\Program Files\..."` returns "The network path was not found" and single-quoted PowerShell filters get mangled. Fighting backticks and nested quotes through the ssh boundary burns many round-trips. The reliable form is: write the script to a local file with `write_file`, `scp` it as `C:/Users/User/x.ps1`, then `ssh … 'powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File C:/Users/User/x.ps1'` (forward slashes in the `-File` path; backslashes get eaten). Delete the script from the host afterwards. This is also how a Go/PowerShell reproduction of the exact failing invocation gets run there — building a throwaway `.exe` with `GOOS=windows` and scp'ing it is faster and more faithful than approximating the call through PowerShell. Confidence: 0.8
- Per-node harness CLI version skew is a real deployment hazard: the Mac was on Command Code 0.52.0 (no `--output-format`, no `--resume`) while the Linux host was on 1.66.0, so the exact same worker args passed locally and died on the Mac with `too many arguments`. Check each node's reported `versions.*` before trusting an E2E that only ran on one host, and `cmd --help` on the target to confirm the flag contract rather than inheriting the local build's. That `versions.*` field is also **noisy on Windows**: the version probe captures stdout+stderr together, so node's `ExperimentalWarning` on stderr lands in the string and the reported version is a warning instead of `1.66.0` — cosmetic, but it means a messy version on Windows is a known artifact rather than fresh skew, and a one-line stderr filter tidies it. Confidence: 0.8
- The kanban review card is rendered by `web/src/features/board/ReviewSection.tsx`, which keys off the `files` + `diff` fields and re-derives names from `diff --git a/X b/Y`; its names must be cross-checked against the backend's authoritative file list. Confidence: 0.65
- The agent resolves the control plane from `NODE_AGENT_SERVER` (via `nodeAgentBase()`), so any test touching `streamCommand`/progress posting must point it at an `httptest` stub or it blocks on the real 10s HTTP timeout to an unreachable address. A timing-sensitive regression test is only trustworthy once you have re-introduced the bug and watched it fail. Confidence: 0.8

Two consequences for the control plane: (a) `resolvedEnv` is only populated from `os.Environ()` when preflight is on, so an explicit env block on a shell job is not a reliable channel; (b) there is **no generic env passthrough** (`NODE_AGENT_ENV`/`ExtraEnv`/`job.Env` don't exist), which is why cross-shell settings have to travel as CLI flags rather than fighting for an env seam. - "Blank screen when I come back to the tab" in this app is a **client-side unmount, not a server fault** — the HTML is served fine, the DOM just ends up empty. Two known producers, both evidence-backed: (1) `web/src` has **no error boundary anywhere** (zero matches for `ErrorBoundary`/`componentDidCatch`/`getDerivedStateFromError`/`Suspense`), so any uncaught render error in React 19 tears down the whole tree — `AuthGate` → `App` → a lazy page chunk die together — and the 16 `React.lazy` page chunks at `web/src/App.tsx:12-27` are the prime throw site (**this was true at diagnosis time; a boundary now exists** — see the chunk-recovery bullet below for `web/src/components/app/app-error-boundary.tsx` and what it now handles); it renders as a featureless dark rectangle rather than an error because `web/src/index.css:134-137` sets `html, body, #root { height: 100% }` + `body { background: var(--color-bg) }`. (2) `web/public/sw.js` precaches only `/`, `/index.html` and the manifest — never the hashed `/assets/*` — and its `event.respondWith(fetch(request).catch(() => caches.match(request).then(c => c || caches.match("/index.html"))))` serves an **HTML document as a JavaScript response** for any uncached asset request, which kills module parsing; the same branch serves a stale cached `index.html` pointing at asset hashes a redeploy deleted. The SW is registered only on https (`web/src/main.tsx:14`), so the default `KANBAN_ADDR` 127.0.0.1:8790 local path is *not* affected — check the protocol before blaming the SW. Discriminator that worked: console shows a JS syntax/MIME error → SW; a React stack trace → missing boundary; Network shows `/index.html` but no `/assets/*` request at all → #3 below. Confidence: 0.7

Needs a single guard/debounce so only one reload happens. Confidence: 0.65

Changing the wrapper's radius via `className` requires updating `--beam-radius` or the corners break again. The `@property` registration needs Chrome/Safari/Firefox 128+; in an older engine the angle never interpolates and the beam sits still. Confidence: 0.75

 to the outer shell; inline `node -e "…"` loses its quotes. The pattern that works: `base64.b64encode(content)` → `[Convert]::FromBase64String` + `[IO.File]::WriteAllBytes` delivered as `powershell -NoProfile -EncodedCommand <base64 of utf-16-le>`, and structured output read back the same way (`[Convert]::ToBase64String([IO.File]::ReadAllBytes(...))` emitted after a `PROBE=`/`B64:` sentinel on its own line) so no escaping is lost in transit. Find the holder with `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 3000 -State Listen` plus `Get-Process -Id $_.OwningProcess` (it will be `node`), kill only that PID, and re-check the port is free before dispatching. The durable config shape is `reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI` so a healthy running app is adopted while a stale server is kept out of real CI — but keep `webServer` defined unconditionally, because the board's dispatch runs with nothing listening and needs Playwright to start the app itself. The two signatures are distinct and both real: `EADDRINUSE` means something else holds the port, `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` means Playwright adopted a server that was already dying. Confidence: 0.8

Load-bearing rule: **never answer a script/asset request with an HTML body** — that fails as a parse/MIME error, not a network error, and React silently never mounts; a failed asset lookup returns a synthetic `504 Offline` text response rather than a wrong-typed cached body. A `fetch(request).catch(() => caches.match(request).then(c => c || caches.match("/index.html")))` catch-all is the anti-pattern. Confidence: 0.7

The stored GitHub token is a **classic `ghp_` PAT and is already dead** (401 from `api.github.com`, so the credential needs replacing, not rotating; classic PATs are deprecated in favour of fine-grained ones). `gh` is logged in separately with `repo` scope. The **remote** GitHub MCP server at `https://api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/` is still the better target than the stdio one (configurable toolsets vs the fixed ~30, no `npx` cold-start per connect) — but **OAuth/DCR does not work against it from hermes**. Verified: `POST https://api.githubcopilot.com/register` → 404, `registration_endpoint` is absent from `https://github.com/login/oauth/.well-known/openid-configuration`, and the advertised `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp/` metadata (note the trailing `/mcp/` — `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` alone 404s) names that same registration-less AS. hermes needs dynamic client registration to run an MCP OAuth flow, so `hermes mcp add github --url … --auth oauth` + `hermes mcp login github` always fail with `Registration failed: 404`; the remote server only works via `--auth header` with a fine-grained PAT, since the metadata advertises `bearer_methods_supported: ["header"]`. Corollary that breaks the usual "add the new path before removing the old one" advice: `hermes mcp add <name>` **overwrites** an existing entry of the same name, and a failed connect is still written to `~/.hermes/config.yaml` marked `(disabled)` — so reusing a working server's name leaves zero working GitHub MCP until it is restored. `@modelcontextprotocol/server-github` (stdio) starts fine with no token at all, for public repos only. Confidence: 0.8
- `cmd/server/main.go` `authHandler` gates on an **`/api/` string prefix**, not route registration — so any non-`/api/` route is served unauthenticated. Put a new user-content route (e.g. extension assets) under `/api/` to inherit the gate for free. The server sets **zero security headers**: no CSP, no `nosniff`, no `X-Frame-Options` — and CSP can't simply be added, because `index.html:12-23` carries an inline blocking theme script. `profileDir` is unexported, so `package main` cannot build profile paths; export a resolver next to `ecosystemStore.path`. `hermes` is a fully opaque subprocess across 3 spawn sites (`ai.go:30`, `chat_exec.go:322`, `ssh_dispatch.go:433`) plus a daemon HTTP path — it is spawned with no config file, no env handoff, and no MCP library exists in `go.sum`, so anything the agent must consume has to be written to a file it will read later. Confidence: 0.75
- `cmd/mcp-server` is the board's own **MCP server** (stdio JSON-RPC, protocol `2024-11-05`, one JSON object per line, no `go get` — the module is `kanban-board` with no domain path), so Claude Code/editors/hermes can query the board. It is deliberately **read-only with no flag that enables mutation**: `readOnlyTools()` is the whole surface and `TestToolSurfaceIsReadOnly` asserts a `forbiddenTools` list (25 mutating domain ops) is absent, so a mutating tool can't be added without failing CI. Permanently excluded for mechanism reasons, not gating reasons: `SaveWorkspace`/`DeleteWorkspace` (validates ID but **not** `Path` or `Host`, and those feed `sh -lc` and `ssh` in `codeGraphCommand` at `codegraph.go:67` — RCE/SSRF), `Approve` (commits and pushes), `ImportBoard`, the `Bulk*`/`ReorderTasks` family, `ClearExecutionHistory`, and the chat/daemon/terminal launchers. `AddComment` (`comments.go:112`) is also not side-effect-free (commenting on a `review` task resets it to `todo`), `NodeAgentHealth` (`nodeagent.go:480`) broadcasts SSE on *every read* so a polled tool amplifies traffic, and `ExportBoard` (`dataops.go:298`) is N+1 DB opens per task. `taskByID` is unexported, so `GetTask` wraps the public `ListTasksQuery(slug, TaskQuery{Q: id, Limit: 5})` path. Reached functions: `ListBoards`, `ListTasksQuery`, `ListComments`, `TaskEvents`, `TaskHealthFor`, `WorkerLogTail`, `ReadLogTail`, `ListWorkspaces`, `CodeGraphReportForWorkspace`, `ListHermesMCPServers`. The profile-scoped config is the sharp edge: a tool arg defaulting to `""` instead of `"default"` silently resolves to nothing and errors on every call. Confidence: 0.8
- **"Failed to fetch dynamically imported module" for a content-hashed chunk is a two-bug chain, and both halves live in the serving path, not in the route.** `spa()` in `cmd/server/main.go` falls through to `index.html` for *any* unmatched path, so once a redeploy deletes an old chunk the request returns **200 OK with a `text/html` body and `Cache-Control: immutable, max-age=31536000`** — proved with a throwaway in-package Go test against `spa(dir)` (`/assets/Missing-XyZ123.js status=200 cache="…immutable" ctype="text/html"`), not inferred from reading the handler. `web/public/sw.js` then makes it permanent: `cachePut` gates only on `response.ok`, so the bogus 200 is written into `ASSET_CACHE` under the `.js` URL, and cache-first + `immutable` means that chunk can never load again — only a manual "Clear site data" clears it. Also in that SW: `VERSION = "switchyard-v2"` is hardcoded and never tied to the build, so the `activate` filter `!key.startsWith(VERSION)` never prunes `ASSET_CACHE` and dead chunks accumulate forever; `skipWaiting` + `clients.claim()` (sw.js:19,30) hand a new SW control of a tab that is already open, which is what makes the bug read as intermittent. `App.tsx` lazy-imports every route with no error boundary, so one dead chunk white-screens instead of recovering. The live site is `https://kanban.adityahimaone.space` — https, so the SW is active there while the default `127.0.0.1:8790` path is not. Audit order that settled it: glob for a reverse-proxy/deploy config first, read the static handler and its cache headers, **diff the hash from the pasted error against the current `web/dist/assets/`** (`WorkspacesPage-CxeKwLT6.js` is gone, `-5U7Foemc.js` is live), read the service worker, then prove the server's real behaviour with the throwaway test and delete it. Confidence: 0.75
- Reference model for a port ask: hermes-webui's extension is a **browser-side injection loader** — serve a dir at `/extensions/`, inject same-origin CSS/JS into a server-rendered `index.html` (no build step), hand extensions `window.hermesExt.register(id)` with settings/storage/turn-event handles, and by its own docs it runs with **full session authority** ("if your WebUI is shared with users you do not fully trust, do not enable extensions"). The board's extension is a server-side capability declaration that gates nothing — same word, different concept. The parts that transfer cleanly: static-file sandbox rules (traversal/encoded-traversal, dotfile, escaping-symlink rejection), an asset-URL allowlist (same-origin, `/extensions/` or `/static/` prefix only, reject scheme/host/fragment/dot-segments) which is the real XSS guard, a `/api/extensions/status` returning only sanitized counts, and an enable/disable override route (which is what the dead `enabled` field wants). Confidence: 0.7
- The VPS is **not headless**, despite the absence-of-evidence probes suggesting it: Ubuntu 24.04 with LightDM running, Xorg live on `:0`, and `systemctl get-default` reporting `graphical.target`. `ls /tmp/.X11-unix` plus a `ss`/services sweep for `vnc|guacamole|ttyd|xrdp` reads as "no desktop"; the decisive checks are `systemctl status display-manager` and `fuser /tmp/.X11-unix/X0` (returns the Xorg PID). So a terminal opened on the box's own desktop *does* use the fonts in `~/.local/share/fonts`, GNOME Terminal is the default emulator there (right-click → Preferences → profile → Custom font), and no one is logged in graphically so that path needs physical/VNC access. The discriminator for which context the user is in is `echo $SSH_CONNECTION`: empty = the VPS desktop itself, an IP like `112.78.177.2` = a remote client. Confidence: 0.8

Also remember a `/index.html` request gets a **301** from `FileServer` normalization, so it is not a valid assertion target for "the shell was served". Full green state at that point: `go build ./...` + `go test ./...`, `pnpm build` (typecheck), and all 51 web tests. Confidence: 0.75
